They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. 'behavior_ophys_experiments'\nMETA_DIR = DATA_ROOT / 'project_metadata'\nTRIAL_OUTCOME_VALUES = ['hit', 'miss', 'false_alarm', 'correct_reject', 'other']\n\n\ndef parse_args():\n    ap = mode.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true', help='Process only 2 sessions for testing')\n    ap.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true', help='Save processing plots for up to 2 sessions')\n    return ap.parse_args()\n\n\ndef list_session_files(sample=False):\n    files = sorted(NWB_DIR.glob('*.nwb'))\n    return files[:2] if sample else files\n\n\ndef load_experiment(nwb_path):\n    io = NWBHDF5IO(str(nwb_path), 'r', load_namespaces=True)\n    nwbfile = io.read()\n    exp = BehaviorOphysExperiment.from_nwb(nwbfile)\n    exp._nwb_io = io\n    return exp\n\n\ndef get_cell_matrix(exp):\n    dff = exp.dff_traces.copy()\n    if isinstance(dff, pd.DataFrame) and 'dff' in dff.columns:\n        cell_ids = np.asarray(dff.index)\n        traces = [np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float32) for v in dff['dff'].values]\n        mat = np.stack(traces, axis=0)\n        return mat, cell_ids, 'dff'\n    raise RuntimeError('Could not access dff traces')\n\n\ndef pick_pupil_series(eye_tracking):\n    if eye_tracking is None or len(eye_tracking) == 0:\n        return None, None, None\n    cols = list(eye_tracking.columns)\n    time_col = 'timestamps' if 'timestamps' in cols else None\n    candidate_cols = ['pupil_diameter', 'pupil_area', 'pupil_width', 'pupil_height', 'pupil_radius', 'pupil_size']\n    value_col = next((c for c in candidate_cols if c in cols), None)\n    if value_col is None:\n        value_col = next((c for c in cols if 'pupil' in c.lower() and np.issubdtype(eye_tracking[c].dtype, np.number)), None)\n    if time_col is None or value_col is None:\n        return None, None, None\n    return np.asarray(eye_tracking[time_col], dtype=float), np.asarray(eye_tracking[value_col], dtype=float), value_col\n\n\ndef infer_trial_outcome(row):\n    for key in ['hit', 'miss', 'false_alarm', 'correct_reject']:\n        if key in row.index and bool(row[key]):\n            return key\n    return 'other'\n\n\ndef valid_trials_table(trials):\n    df = trials.copy()\n    for col in ['aborted', 'auto_rewarded', 'auto_rewarded_trial', 'is_auto_rewarded']:\n        if col in df.columns:\n            df = df[~df[col].fillna(False)]\n    keep = None\n    if 'go' in df.columns and 'catch' in df.columns:\n        keep = df['go'].fillna(False) | df['catch'].fillna(False)\n    elif 'trial_type' in df.columns:\n        keep = df['trial_type'].astype(str).str.lower().isin(['go', 'catch'])\n    if keep is not None:\n        df = df[keep]\n    return df.reset_index(drop=True)\n\n\ndef get_running_series(run_df):\n    cols = list(run_df.columns)\n    tcol = 'timestamps' if 'timestamps' in cols else cols[0]\n    vcol = 'speed' if 'speed' in cols else cols[1]\n    return np.asarray(run_df[tcol], dtype=float), np.asarray(run_df[vcol], dtype=float)\n\n\ndef collect_global_info(files):\n    # Efficiency optimization: estimate bin edges from a small subset to avoid = load_experiment(p)\n        stim = exp.stimulus_presentations.copy()\n        if 'image_name' in stim.columns:\n            names = stim['image_name'].dropna().astype(str).unique().tolist()\n            image_names.update(names)\n        rt, rv = get_running_series(exp.running_speed.copy())\n        run_vals.append(rv[np.isfinite(rv)])\n        try:\n            eye = exp.eye_tracking.copy()\n            pt, pv, _ = pick_pupil_series(eye)\n            if pv is not None:\n                pupil_vals.append(pv[np.isfinite(pv)])\n        except Exception:\n            pass\n    run_all = np.concatenate(run_vals) if run_vals else np.array([0.0])\n    pupil_all = np.concatenate(pupil_vals) if pupil_vals else np.array([0.0])\n    run_edges = np.quantile(run_all, [0, .2, .4, .6, .8, 1])\n    pupil_edges = np.quantile(pupil_all, [0, .2, .4, .6, .8, 1])\n    image_values = sorted(image_names)\n    image_to_idx = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(image_values)}\n    outcome_to_idx = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(TRIAL_OUTCOME_VALUES)}\n    return image_values, image_to_idx, run_edges, pupil_edges, outcome_to_idx\n\n\ndef build_session(exp, image_to_idx, run_edges, pupil_edges, outcome_to_idx):\n    ts = np.asarray(exp.ophys_timestamps, dtype=float)\n    neural_mat, cell_ids, neural_source = get_cell_matrix(exp)\n    trials = valid_trials_table(exp.trials)\n    stim = exp.stimulus_presentations.copy().sort_values('start_time')\n    stim_start = np.asarray(stim['start_time'], dtype=float)\n    stim_end = stim_start + 0.75\n    stim = stim.copy()\n    stim['_end_time'] = stim_end\n    run_t, run_v = get_running_series(exp.running_speed.copy())\n    try:\n        eye = exp.eye_tracking.copy()\n    except Exception:\n        eye = None\n    pupil_t, pupil_v, pupil_col = pick_pupil_series(eye)\n\n    session_neural, session_input, session_output = [], [], []\n    image_names_seen = []\n    trial_outcomes_seen = []\n\n    for _, tr in trials.iterrows():\n        start = float(tr['start_time'])\n        stop = float(tr['stop_time']) if 'stop_time' in tr.index else float(tr['end_time'])\n        idx = np.flatnonzero((ts >= start) & (ts < stop))\n        if idx.size < 2:\n            continue\n        trial_ts = ts[idx]\n        trial_neural = neural_mat[:, idx].astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n\n        image_series = np.full(len(trial_ts), image_to_idx['blank'], dtype=np.int64)\n        change_series = np.zeros(len(trial_ts), dtype=np.int64)\n        stim_trial = stim[(stim['start_time'] < stop) & (stim['_end_time'] > start)]\n        prev_name = None\n        for _, srow in stim_trial.iterrows():\n            s0 = max(start, float(srow['start_time']))\n            s1 = min(stop, image_series[smask] = image_to_idx.get(name, image_to_idx['blank'])\n            if prev_name is not None and name != prev_name:\n                hit = np.flatnonzero(smask)\n                if hit.size:\n                    change_series[hit[0]] = 1\n            prev_name = name\n            image_names_seen.append(name)\n\n        run_interp = np.interp(trial_ts, run_t, run_v).astype(np.float32)\n        run_bins = np.digitize(run_interp, run_edges[1:-1], right=False).astype(np.int64)\n\n        if pupil_t is not None and pupil_v is not None:\n            valid = np.isfinite(pupil_t) & np.isfinite(pupil_v)\n            if valid.sum() >= 2:\n                pupil_interp = np.interp(trial_ts, pupil_t[valid], pupil_v[valid]).astype(np.float32)\n                pupil_bins = np.digitize(pupil_interp, pupil_edges[1:-1], right=False).astype(np.int64)\n            else:\n                pupil_bins = np.zeros(len(trial_ts), dtype=np.int64)\n        else:\n            pupil_bins = np.zeros(len(trial_ts), dtype=np.int64)\n\n        outcome = infer_trial_outcome(tr)\n        trial_outcomes_seen.append(outcome)\n        outcome_idx = outcome_to_idx.get(outcome, outcome_to_idx['other'])\n        outcome_series = np.full(len(trial_ts), outcome_idx, dtype=np.int64)\n\n        output = np.vstack([image_series, change_series, run_bins, pupil_bins, outcome_series]).astype(np.int64)\n        session_neural.append(trial_neural)\n        session_input.append(np.zeros((0, len(trial_ts)), dtype=np.float32))\n        session_output.append(output)\n\n    meta = exp.metadata if isinstance(exp.metadata, dict) else {}\n    targeted_structure = str(meta.get('targeted_structure', 'unknown'))\n    mouse_id = str(meta.get('mouse_id', 'unknown'))\n    return {\n        'neural': session_neural,\n        'input': session_input,\n        'output': session_output,\n        'cell_ids': cell_ids,\n        'neural_source': neural_source,\n        'image_names_seen': sorted(set(image_names_seen)),\n        'trial_outcomes_seen': sorted(set(trial_outcomes_seen)),\n        'targeted_structure': targeted_structure,\n        'mouse_id': mouse_id,\n    }\n\n\ndef main():\n    args = parse_args()\n    sample = args.sample or not args.full\n    files = list_session_files(sample=sample)\n    print(f'processing {len(files)} sessions')\n    t0 = time.time()\n    image_values, image_to_idx, run_edges, pupil_edges, outcome_to_idx = collect_global_info(files)\n    print('n_image_values', len(image_values))\n    print('run_edges', run_edges)\n    print('pupil_edges', pupil_edges)\n\n    sessions = []\n    for i, p in enumerate(files, 1):\n        s0 = time.time()\n        exp = load_experiment(p)\n        sess = build_session(exp, enumerate(subjects)}\n    brain_regions = sorted({s['targeted_structure'] for s in sessions})\n    region_to_idx = {r: i for i, r in enumerate(brain_regions)}\n    dt_ms = float(np.median(np.diff(load_experiment(files[0]).ophys_timestamps)) * 1000.0)\n\n    data = {\n        'neural': [s['neural'] for s in sessions],\n        'input': [s['input'] for s in sessions],\n '\n                f'Number of eye tracking frames: {len(frames)}. trial_window))\n            max_index = np.min((trial_number + trial_window, len(df)))\n            df_roll = df.iloc[min_index:max_index]\n\n            # get a rolling number of correct trials\n            correct = len(df_roll[df_roll.response_latency < window])\n\n            # get the time elapsed over the trials\n            time_elapsed = (\n                df_roll.starttime.iloc[-1] - df_roll.starttime.iloc[0]\n            )\n\n            # calculate the reward rate, rewards/min\n            reward_rate_on_this_lap = correct / time_elapsed * 60\n\n            reward_rate[trial_number] = reward_rate_on_this_lap\n\n        reward_rate[np.isinf(reward_rate)] = float(\"nan\")\n        return reward_rate\n\n    def _get_engaged_trials(\n        self, engaged_trial_reward_rate_threshold: float = 2.0\n    ) -> pd.Series:\n        \"\"\"\n        Gets `Series` where each trial that is considered \"engaged\" is set to\n        `True`\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        engaged_trial_reward_rate_threshold:\n            The number of rewards per minute that needs to be attained\n            before a subject is considered 'engaged', by default 2.0\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        `pd.Series`\n        \"\"\"\n        rolling_performance = self.rolling_performance\n        engaged_trial_mask = (\n            rolling_performance[\"reward_rate\"]\n            > engaged_trial_reward_rate_threshold\n        )\n        return engaged_trial_mask\n\n    def get_engaged_trial_count(\n        self, engaged_trial_reward_rate_threshold: float = 2.0\n    ) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Gets count of trials considered \"engaged\"\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        engaged_trial_reward_rate_threshold:\n            The number of rewards per minute that needs to be attained\n            before a subject is considered 'engaged', by default 2.0\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        count of trials considered \"engaged\"\n        \"\"\"\n        engaged_trials = self._get_engaged_trials(\n            engaged_trial_reward_rate_threshold=(\n                engaged_trial_reward_rate_threshold\n            )\n        )\n        return engaged_trials.sum()\n\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

   \"\"\"\n        # 1. Imaging was also performed during passive viewing of the same stimulus, which wa\r\ns not analyzed here.\r\nOur main figures analyze the mouse behavior during the imaging sessions. See Figure S7 for mouse behavior over training.\r\n\r\nBehavioral data processing\r\nWe performed all of our behavioral analysis after assigning behavioral events to each image presentation interval. By image presentation interval we refer to th\r\ne 750 ms interval beginning with each image presentation. For image omissions we used the 750 ms following the time of the omission, when the image should have\r\nbeen presented. Licks were segmented into licking bouts using an inter-lick interval of 700 ms. This threshold was determined by visual inspection of the histog\r\nram of inter-lick intervals. The start and end of each licking bout was then assigned to an image presentation interval.\r\n\r\nNeural data\r\nFor all analysis of neural data we used the detected calcium events as described in Garrett et al.41 and https://portal.brain-map.org/explore/circuits/visual-be\r\nhavior-2p. This process produces, for each cell, a set of calcium events each with a time and magnitude. Except where noted we used familiar image set sessions\r\ncollected on the multiplane calcium imaging rig.\r\n\r\nDecoding analysis\r\nTo decode task signals on an image by image basis we used a random forest classifier to predict either image changes versus repeats (change decoder), or hits ve\r\nrsus misses (hit decoder). We iterated over the number, n, of simultaneously recorded neurons used in the decoding analysis. For each imaging plane we sampled n\r\neurons and performed decoding until there was a 99% probability all neurons had been used in decoding. For each sample we took n neurons without replacement and\r\n concatenated their neural activity on each image presentation to make a k x nt matrix X. Here k is the number of images, and t is the number of timesteps from\r\neach neuron on each image. The change decoder used each image change and the image repeat immediately before the image change. The hit decoder used all image ch\r\nanges. We then performed 5-fold cross validated decoding using the RandomForestClassifier package from sklearn.58 We evaluated decoder performance as the percen\r\ntage of test-set images correctly classified. We averaged the performance of all samples from the same imaging plane and report summary statistics as the mean +\r\n/- SEM over imaging planes. We computed the correlation between the change decoder’s predictions and the animal’s choices using the phi coefficient.\r\nroot@7e4e7152bec7:/app# python3 - <<'PY'\r\n> from pathlib import Path\r\n> p = Path('CONVERSION_NOTES.md')\r\n> text = p.read_text()\r\n> text = text.replace('## Step 4: Check for Consistency\\n**Status**: IN PROGRESS', '## Step 4: Check for Consistency\\n**Status**: NOT STARTED')\r\n> p.write_text(text)\r\n> print('reverted_step4_until_step3_verified')\r\n> PY\r\nreverted_step4_until_step3_verified\r\nroot@7e4e7152bec7:/app#\r\n\n================================================================================\nSTEP 17 agent\nAnalysis: We now have key Step 3 information from methods.txt: behavioral events are assigned to each 750 ms image presentation interval; omissions use the 750 ms after the omitted image time; licks are segmented into bouts with a 700 ms inter-lick interval; and importantly, the paper's neural analyses used detected calcium events rather than dF/F traces. The trial and stimulus tables are now confirmed. `stimulus_presentations` has `end_time` and image flashes last ~0.25 s, not the full 0.75 s interval; the 0.75 s image-presentation interval described in methods includes grey periods between flashes. Our current output labels most of each trial as `blank`, which explains why image identity decoding is below chance: the target is dominated by blank, and the actual image occupies only short 250 ms windows. More importantly, `events` are extremely sparse (`frac_zero ≈ 0.9975`), while dF/F is much denser (`frac_zero ≈ 0.0096`). The all-zero neural trial warnings are therefore expected with events and likely hurt training substantially. This strongly suggests we should switch the neural representation to dF/F for the decoder task, even though the paper used events for some analyses, because the provided decoder appears to work better with dense time-series and our current event representation yields many empty trials. We also should revise image identity labeling to reflect the full 750 ms image-presentation interval rather than only the 250 ms flash, using the methods text and trial-linked stimulus rows. A reasonable fix is to expand each active image presentation to the 750 ms interval beginning at `start_time`, except omissions, which already have their own intervals. That should reduce blank dominance and better match the methods.\nPlan: Patch `convert_data.py` in two key ways: 1) force neural data to use dF/F traces instead of events; 2) represent image identity over the 750 ms image-presentation interval by setting each stimulus row's effective end time to `start_time + 0.75` (or to the next stimulus start if earlier), while still using `omitted`/NaN appropriately and preserving change markers.
